5962: Theorem 8.46 (Euclidean covariance and Poincaré reconstruction). The Schwinger family satisfies the
5963: full O(4) covariance axiom. Together with reflection positivity, permutation symmetry, temperedness
5964: and continuous translations, this gives by the Osterwalder–Schrader reconstruction theorem a strongly
5965: continuous unitary representation of the proper orthochronous Poincaré group on the reconstructed Hilbert
5966: space, with translation spectrum in the closed forward cone. Spatial rotations and translations are obtained
5967: directly on the OS quotient; Lorentz boosts arise from analytic continuation of the Euclidean rotation
5968: covariance.
5969: Proof. Theorem 8.45 is precisely the Euclidean covariance identity required for every finite tensor/source
5970: multiplet, and the nested source construction extends it to the complete chosen local field family. The
5971: direct OS theorem, Lemma 8.14, and Lemma 8.13 supply the remaining reconstruction hypotheses,
5972: including the linear-growth condition. The standard Osterwalder–Schrader covariance reconstruction
5973: then produces the positive- energy Poincaré representation. No unitary action of a Euclidean rotation
5974: that changes the reflection hyperplane is assumed on the intermediate OS quotient.

5976: 9     Continuum reconstruction and spectral gap
5977: 9.1    Osterwalder–Schrader reconstruction and clustering gap
5978: The common Schwinger functional Let D be the countable algebra generated by positive-time
5979: translates of the bounded positive-scale cylinders and the normalized finite local source fields constructed
5980: by the marked hierarchy. The multiscale source-tree theorem supplies a projective Schwinger functional
5981: S on D with the following properties:
5982: (S1) temperedness and continuity in every Schwartz test tensor;
5983: (S2) stationarity, gauge invariance, permutation symmetry, and reflection positivity;
5984: (S3) continuous Euclidean translations;
5985: (S4) for every two field polynomials F, G with separated supports,
5986: 
5987:                               |S(F G) − S(F )S(G)| ≤ CF,G e−m dist1 (supp F,supp G)                   (166)
5988: 
5989:      with one m > 0 independent of the chosen polynomials;
5990: (S5) the OS linear-growth estimate of Lemma 8.13 on every finite source multiplet.
5991: The constants CF,G may depend on the fixed fields and test functions; the exponent m does not.
5992: Theorem 9.1 (Continuous OS reconstruction). Let Θ be time reflection and D+ the positive-time
5993: subalgebra. The form
5994:                                         ⟨F, G⟩OS = S(ΘF G)
5995: is positive semidefinite. Its quotient and completion give a separable Hilbert space H, vacuum Ω = [1],
5996: and a strongly continuous self-adjoint contraction semigroup
5997: 
5998:                                          T (t) = e−tH ,    H ≥ 0.
5999: 
6000: Proof. Reflection positivity gives the positive form and the null ideal N = {F : ∥F ∥OS = 0}. Positive-time
6001: translations preserve D+ for sufficiently small translations on each compactly supported word and define
6002: contractions on the quotient; the usual time-zero limiting argument extends them to all t ≥ 0. For a core
6003: vector [F ],
6004:                                         2
6005:                       ∥T (t)[F ] − [F ]∥ = S(ΘF τ2t F ) − 2ℜS(ΘF τt F ) + S(ΘF F ),
6006: which tends to zero by translation continuity. Contractivity and density give strong continuity on H.
6007: Reflection symmetry makes the semigroup self-adjoint, and the spectral theorem gives T (t) = e−tH with
6008: H ≥ 0.

6032: Theorem 9.3 (Unique vacuum and complete local mass gap). The OS Hamiltonian satisfies
6033: 
6034:                                                H Ω⊥ ≥ m > 0.                                          (169)
6035: 
6036: The vacuum is unique.
6037: Proof. The centered classes [F ] − S(F )Ω, F ∈ D+ , are dense in Ω⊥ . Lemma 9.2 says that the spectral
6038: projection EH ([0, m − ε]) annihilates this dense set for every ε > 0; hence it vanishes on Ω⊥ . This proves
6039: the absence of spectrum in (0, m).
6040:    For a centered core vector, (168) tends to zero, so its spectral measure has no atom at zero. Density
6041: then shows that EH ({0})H = CΩ. Thus the vacuum is unique and (169) holds.

6043: 10     Nontriviality and finite spectral window
6044: 10.1     Endpoint interpolation and reflected noncollapse
6045: Let Yr (f ) be the tangent-normalized, Wick-centered scalar curvature source at physical scale r, with the
6046: source root parallel transported in the normalized finite-jet frame. The test profile f is chosen once at
6047: unit scale and dilated with the source so that its dimensionless support remains fixed and lies strictly in
6048: positive Euclidean time.
6049: Lemma 10.1 (Positive Gaussian OS norm). There is such a profile f for which
6050: 
6051:                                     V0 (f ) := S0 ΘYr (f ) Yr (f ) > 0.
6052:                                                                   
6053: 
6054: 
6055: The value is independent of r in the normalized frame.
6056: Proof. At the Gaussian fixed point the centered scalar curvature composite creates a two-curvature Fock
6057: vector. In Fourier variables its squared norm is an integral of the squared symmetrized two-particle
6058: amplitude against the positive transverse curvature spectral density. That density is nonzero on an open
6059: set, so choosing a positive-time profile whose Fourier transform is nonzero there gives a strictly positive
6060: integral. Reflection positivity identifies the integral with the displayed OS form. Scale covariance of the
6061: tangent-normalized source makes the value independent of r.

6032: Theorem 9.3 (Unique vacuum and complete local mass gap). The OS Hamiltonian satisfies
6033: 
6034:                                                H Ω⊥ ≥ m > 0.                                          (169)

6036: The vacuum is unique.
6037: Proof. The centered classes [F ] − S(F )Ω, F ∈ D+ , are dense in Ω⊥ . Lemma 9.2 says that the spectral
6038: projection EH ([0, m − ε]) annihilates this dense set for every ε > 0; hence it vanishes on Ω⊥ . This proves

6041: then shows that EH ({0})H = CΩ. Thus the vacuum is unique and (169) holds.

6043: 10     Nontriviality and finite spectral window
6044: 10.1     Endpoint interpolation and reflected noncollapse
6045: Let Yr (f ) be the tangent-normalized, Wick-centered scalar curvature source at physical scale r, with the
6046: source root parallel transported in the normalized finite-jet frame. The test profile f is chosen once at
6047: unit scale and dilated with the source so that its dimensionless support remains fixed and lies strictly in
6048: positive Euclidean time.
6049: Lemma 10.1 (Positive Gaussian OS norm). There is such a profile f for which
6050: 
6051:                                     V0 (f ) := S0 ΘYr (f ) Yr (f ) > 0.
6052:                                                                   
6053: 
6054: 
6055: The value is independent of r in the normalized frame.
6056: Proof. At the Gaussian fixed point the centered scalar curvature composite creates a two-curvature Fock
6057: vector. In Fourier variables its squared norm is an integral of the squared symmetrized two-particle
6058: amplitude against the positive transverse curvature spectral density. That density is nonzero on an open
6059: set, so choosing a positive-time profile whose Fourier transform is nonzero there gives a strictly positive
6060: integral. Reflection positivity identifies the integral with the displayed OS form. Scale covariance of the
6061: tangent-normalized source makes the value independent of r.
6062:

6205: Theorem 10.4 (Endpoint packet-shell supplier). For the graph-segment family constructed above, the
6206: complete twice-marked endpoint expansion has the following properties.
6207:     (i) The grounded periodic covariance has volume-independent AA, AF , and F F rows of orders two,
6208:         three, and four. After the exact global contact is subtracted, every complete reflection/BRS packet
6209:         has two lattice-difference factors and therefore gains (r/ℓi )2 at weak scale i.
6210:    (ii) The exact weak recurrence admits a finite crossing-cell decomposition. On each fixed cell, both g
6211:         derivatives are taken before the labelled large-field partition and before bad-core ownership. The
6212:         differentiated partition is then recollected with the same charged events and with no same-owner
6213:         cancellation assumption.
6214:  (iii) Before crossing, the full rooted stable map, including wrapping and charged-flat coordinates, is C 2
6215:         in g with a strictly contracting same-order marked row. At first crossing, the fresh marked family
6216:         lies in a uniform hybrid pivot–jet polydisc and satisfies the original-link Fourier–Wiener incidence
6217:         row required by the joint Haar–optical collect.
6218:   (iv) The crossing contribution and the no-crossing terminal contribution are controlled by one geometri-
6219:         cally summable conditional-packet shell majorant. The same majorant controls the functional and
6220:         its first two g derivatives, extends to the closed crossing cells, and sends the terminal remainder to
6221:         zero uniformly as the terminal depth tends to infinity.
6222: Consequently the complete normalized two-root functional, with or without one fixed dimension-six defect
6223: root, has a cutoff-, volume-, crossing-cell-, and terminal-depth-independent C 2 bound on 0 ≤ g ≤ gr .
6224: Proof. Appendix A gives the full proof inside this manuscript. Its five subsections establish the grounded-
6225: torus covariance and globally subtracted packet division; the weak crossing recurrence and wrapping rows;
6226: the twice-marked conditional-packet contraction; the pre-owner hybrid-polydisc Wiener and compact-
6227: entrance estimates; and the terminal-shell summation. No additional mathematical input is imported
6228: beyond the results proved in Sections 3–7.

6208:         three, and four. After the exact global contact is subtracted, every complete reflection/BRS packet
6209:         has two lattice-difference factors and therefore gains (r/ℓi )2 at weak scale i.
6210:    (ii) The exact weak recurrence admits a finite crossing-cell decomposition. On each fixed cell, both g
6211:         derivatives are taken before the labelled large-field partition and before bad-core ownership. The
6212:         differentiated partition is then recollected with the same charged events and with no same-owner
6213:         cancellation assumption.
6214:  (iii) Before crossing, the full rooted stable map, including wrapping and charged-flat coordinates, is C 2
6215:         in g with a strictly contracting same-order marked row. At first crossing, the fresh marked family
6216:         lies in a uniform hybrid pivot–jet polydisc and satisfies the original-link Fourier–Wiener incidence
6217:         row required by the joint Haar–optical collect.
6218:   (iv) The crossing contribution and the no-crossing terminal contribution are controlled by one geometri-
6219:         cally summable conditional-packet shell majorant. The same majorant controls the functional and
6220:         its first two g derivatives, extends to the closed crossing cells, and sends the terminal remainder to
6221:         zero uniformly as the terminal depth tends to infinity.
6222: Consequently the complete normalized two-root functional, with or without one fixed dimension-six defect
6223: root, has a cutoff-, volume-, crossing-cell-, and terminal-depth-independent C 2 bound on 0 ≤ g ≤ gr .

6348: 11     Completion for arbitrary fixed compact simple gauge groups
6349: Theorem 11.1 (Completion of the fixed-group construction). For every fixed compact connected simple
6350: G and faithful unitary representation ρ, the group register CG is finite and all hypotheses consumed by
6351: Sections 2–10 are satisfied. Consequently Theorem 1.1 holds.
6352: Proof. Compactness and the analytic bi-invariant geometry give the Karcher, logarithm, coarea, and
6353: faithful-action constants of Section 2. The complete folded block symbol is spacetime-only and tensors
6354: with Ig , so the strict right-inverse and optical coercivity constants are unchanged. The chart complement
6355: is charged by the faithful Wilson action and the irregular activity is exponentially small. The permanent
6356: weak map, centered graph, and BPHZL packet use the same finite-dimensional Lie-algebra tensor calculus,
6357: with the universal color factor CA (G).
6358:    The Peter–Weyl spectral sum converges at the fixed order sG , and the higher hybrid-polydisc argument
6359: supplies the one-pivot nuclear and multipivot Fourier–Wiener rows. The ordinary-periodic toron, seam,
6360: reflection, and wrapping templates are admitted by Theorem D.3; no twisted sector or finite-volume bundle
6361: sum is used. The invariant-jet theorem supplies the complete low-dimensional mixing and dimension-six
6362: regulator spaces. The joint Haar–optical forest, source transport, Ward hierarchy, OS reconstruction,
6363: and noncollapse theorem then apply with constants depending on G but uniform in cutoff, volume, and
6364: directed depth. Appendix F, specifically Table 3, records every independent entry of CG , the first proof
6365: call at which its G-dependence enters, and the subsequent consumers. Thus no additional estimate
6366: uniform in G is hidden in this completion step: after those finite calls, each remaining mechanism is
6367: color-free, tensors with Ig , or takes a finite maximum in a registered finite-dimensional space.

6100: Lemma 10.3 (Existence of the graph-segment interpolation). After choosing r in the common weak
6101: corridor, the segment 0 ≤ g ≤ gr lies in the same real marked domain. At finite cutoff and volume,
6102: evaluate the exact interaction at (g 2 , R⋆ (g 2 )) and express its quadratic part relative to the fixed g = 0
6103: grounded Gaussian. This gives a real C 2 family Vg,r with positive finite normalization. In fixed Gaussian

6109: Here n is field degree. The Wilson row attains the lower exponent ν = n − 2; Haar, chart, coarea,
6110: determinant, and graph-return rows carry additional nonnegative powers. The grading is read only after
6111: each complete BRS/reflection packet is collected. The graph is C 3 in u, so g 7→ (g 2 , R⋆ (g 2 )) is C 2 at
6112: g = 0. The Wilson word, Haar density, normal return, coarea, determinant, and finite Schur endpoint are
6113: analytic in the physical field A = ga. After the Gaussian quadratic form is removed, a Wilson term of field

6205: Theorem 10.4 (Endpoint packet-shell supplier). For the graph-segment family constructed above, the
6206: complete twice-marked endpoint expansion has the following properties.
6207:     (i) The grounded periodic covariance has volume-independent AA, AF , and F F rows of orders two,
6208:         three, and four. After the exact global contact is subtracted, every complete reflection/BRS packet
6209:         has two lattice-difference factors and therefore gains (r/ℓi )2 at weak scale i.
6210:    (ii) The exact weak recurrence admits a finite crossing-cell decomposition. On each fixed cell, both g
6211:         derivatives are taken before the labelled large-field partition and before bad-core ownership. The
6212:         differentiated partition is then recollected with the same charged events and with no same-owner
6213:         cancellation assumption.
6214:  (iii) Before crossing, the full rooted stable map, including wrapping and charged-flat coordinates, is C 2
6215:         in g with a strictly contracting same-order marked row. At first crossing, the fresh marked family
6216:         lies in a uniform hybrid pivot–jet polydisc and satisfies the original-link Fourier–Wiener incidence
6217:         row required by the joint Haar–optical collect.
6218:   (iv) The crossing contribution and the no-crossing terminal contribution are controlled by one geometri-
6219:         cally summable conditional-packet shell majorant. The same majorant controls the functional and
6220:         its first two g derivatives, extends to the closed crossing cells, and sends the terminal remainder to
6221:         zero uniformly as the terminal depth tends to infinity.
6222: Consequently the complete normalized two-root functional, with or without one fixed dimension-six defect
6223: root, has a cutoff-, volume-, crossing-cell-, and terminal-depth-independent C 2 bound on 0 ≤ g ≤ gr .
6224: Proof. Appendix A gives the full proof inside this manuscript. Its five subsections establish the grounded-
6225: torus covariance and globally subtracted packet division; the weak crossing recurrence and wrapping rows;
6226: the twice-marked conditional-packet contraction; the pre-owner hybrid-polydisc Wiener and compact-
6227: entrance estimates; and the terminal-shell summation. No additional mathematical input is imported
6228: beyond the results proved in Sections 3–7.

6032: Theorem 9.3 (Unique vacuum and complete local mass gap). The OS Hamiltonian satisfies
6033: 
6034:                                                H Ω⊥ ≥ m > 0.                                          (169)

6036: The vacuum is unique.
6037: Proof. The centered classes [F ] − S(F )Ω, F ∈ D+ , are dense in Ω⊥ . Lemma 9.2 says that the spectral
6038: projection EH ([0, m − ε]) annihilates this dense set for every ε > 0; hence it vanishes on Ω⊥ . This proves
6039: the absence of spectrum in (0, m).

6041: then shows that EH ({0})H = CΩ. Thus the vacuum is unique and (169) holds.

6044: 10.1     Endpoint interpolation and reflected noncollapse
6045: Let Yr (f ) be the tangent-normalized, Wick-centered scalar curvature source at physical scale r, with the
6046: source root parallel transported in the normalized finite-jet frame. The test profile f is chosen once at
6047: unit scale and dilated with the source so that its dimensionless support remains fixed and lies strictly in
6048: positive Euclidean time.
6049: Lemma 10.1 (Positive Gaussian OS norm). There is such a profile f for which
6050: 
6051:                                     V0 (f ) := S0 ΘYr (f ) Yr (f ) > 0.
6052:                                                                   
6053: 
6054: 
6055: The value is independent of r in the normalized frame.
6056: Proof. At the Gaussian fixed point the centered scalar curvature composite creates a two-curvature Fock
6057: vector. In Fourier variables its squared norm is an integral of the squared symmetrized two-particle
6058: amplitude against the positive transverse curvature spectral density. That density is nonzero on an open
6059: set, so choosing a positive-time profile whose Fourier transform is nonzero there gives a strictly positive
6060: integral. Reflection positivity identifies the integral with the displayed OS form. Scale covariance of the
6061: tangent-normalized source makes the value independent of r.

6348: 11     Completion for arbitrary fixed compact simple gauge groups
6349: Theorem 11.1 (Completion of the fixed-group construction). For every fixed compact connected simple
6350: G and faithful unitary representation ρ, the group register CG is finite and all hypotheses consumed by
6351: Sections 2–10 are satisfied. Consequently Theorem 1.1 holds.
6352: Proof. Compactness and the analytic bi-invariant geometry give the Karcher, logarithm, coarea, and
6353: faithful-action constants of Section 2. The complete folded block symbol is spacetime-only and tensors
6354: with Ig , so the strict right-inverse and optical coercivity constants are unchanged. The chart complement
6355: is charged by the faithful Wilson action and the irregular activity is exponentially small. The permanent
6356: weak map, centered graph, and BPHZL packet use the same finite-dimensional Lie-algebra tensor calculus,
6357: with the universal color factor CA (G).
6358:    The Peter–Weyl spectral sum converges at the fixed order sG , and the higher hybrid-polydisc argument
6359: supplies the one-pivot nuclear and multipivot Fourier–Wiener rows. The ordinary-periodic toron, seam,
6360: reflection, and wrapping templates are admitted by Theorem D.3; no twisted sector or finite-volume bundle
6361: sum is used. The invariant-jet theorem supplies the complete low-dimensional mixing and dimension-six
6362: regulator spaces. The joint Haar–optical forest, source transport, Ward hierarchy, OS reconstruction,
6363: and noncollapse theorem then apply with constants depending on G but uniform in cutoff, volume, and
6364: directed depth. Appendix F, specifically Table 3, records every independent entry of CG , the first proof
6365: call at which its G-dependence enters, and the subsequent consumers. Thus no additional estimate
6366: uniform in G is hidden in this completion step: after those finite calls, each remaining mechanism is
6367: color-free, tensors with Ig , or takes a finite maximum in a registered finite-dimensional space.
6368: Corollary 11.2 (The SU(N ) family). For every fixed integer N ≥ 2, the theorem holds for G = SU(N )
6369: in the defining representation, and the scale-two map is
6370:                                                11N log 2
6371:                              u(2L) = u(L) +              u(L)2 + ON (u(L)3 ).
6372:                                                  24π 2
6373: No estimate uniform as N → ∞ is asserted.
6374: Remark 11.3 (Logical scope). The construction is a fixed-group theorem. The directed subsequence,
6375: curvature-jet observable scope, and absence of a large-rank uniformity claim are part of the statement,
6376: not omitted conclusions.

6453: Lemma A.4 (g-marked one-scale admission). There is g0 > 0 such that, for real 0 ≤ g ≤ g0 , the complete
6454: normalized interaction and all declared source/defect marks are twice continuously differentiable in the
6455: permanent regulator space. An undistinguished non-Gaussian child has parent-normalized row
6456: 
6457:                                                  η0 (g) ≤ Cg,
6458: 
6459: while a child carrying one or two distinguished g derivatives has row
6460: 
6461:                                               η1 (g) + η2 (g) ≤ C.

6463: The exactly recombined large-field/irregular row and its first two g derivatives extend continuously by zero
6464:                                              2
6465: at g = 0 and are bounded by Cm g −pm e−c/g for m ≤ 2. No claim that an attached odd child is O(g 2 ) is
6466: made.
6467: Proof. In the scale-normalized Gaussian coordinate, the cubic Yang–Mills species starts at order g, the
6468: quartic and other non-Gaussian even species start at order g 2 , and the common species tube controls
6469: all field and source marks. Thus an unmarked species row is Cg, while one or two explicit g derivatives
6470: remove at most the corresponding powers and give a finite row. The regulator allocations are additive
6471: under multiplication, and the Gram and Pruefer estimates in the summed replica–BKAR theorem are
6472: insensitive to the parity of an attached tensor. The ordinary parent-normalized child recursion therefore
6473: closes for Cg0 below its printed threshold, with two fixed derivative marks handled by Leibniz. The
6474: C 3 -flat irregular estimate in u = g 2 gives the displayed first and second g-derivative rows after the chain
6475: rule. In particular the model child gA3 differentiated once by a covariance edge is correctly admitted as
6476: an O(g) child, and one g derivative of that child is O(1).

6740: tiate the exact factorization of Definition A.6 with respect to the two curvature roots and at most two
6741: copies of g. The result is a finite sum of complete scale-labelled packet trees such that:

6751: Banach-valued Faà di Bruno formula to the finite composition. This assertion is coefficientwise and uses
6752: only finite-volume differentiability; the uniform compact marked bound is supplied later by Theorem A.20.

6754: one and two g marks, and finite source transport controls the external roots. Uniqueness of the lowest
6755: common ancestor is the ordinary tree property.

6574: scheduled torus and in the rooted thermodynamic step, pair a history whenever its canonically reported
6575: hull has an injective periodic lift and contains no charged atlas label. The local Karcher, Gaussian, coarea,
6576: determinant, localization, extraction and collect templates are then literally the same, so paired histories
6577: and their derivatives cancel coefficientwise. An unpaired regular history has a nontrivial periodic cycle;
6578: its reported hull has length at least cMi . Weakening the permanent rooted support exponent once gives
6579: Ce−σ0 Mi . An unpaired charged history belongs to the exactly recombined U4 row and is Cu−P e−c0 /u .
6580:    For (193), insert H as one distinguished input atom before the connected logarithm. The permanent
6581: one-step derivative envelope is a rooted absolute sum with that atom retained once. The same periodic-lift
6582: pairing applies, and the long hull still pays e−σ0 Mi ; no derivative can shorten the already assigned reported
6583: hull. Vacuum, two-jet and coupling extraction are finite-range linear maps and are paired in the same way.

6739: Lemma A.11 (Exact finite-depth twice-marked conditional forest). At finite directed depth M , differen-
6740: tiate the exact factorization of Definition A.6 with respect to the two curvature roots and at most two

6748: No infinite-depth passage is asserted in this lemma.
6749: Proof. Apply the joint-cumulant derivative identity to every exact normalized weak conditional and, on
6750: a crossing cell, to the exact finite macrobridge and joint compact–optical logarithm. Then apply the
6751: Banach-valued Faà di Bruno formula to the finite composition. This assertion is coefficientwise and uses
6752: only finite-volume differentiability; the uniform compact marked bound is supplied later by Theorem A.20.
6753: The one-step summed replica–BKAR theorem controls each weak packet, Lemma A.4 controls zero,
6754: one and two g marks, and finite source transport controls the external roots. Uniqueness of the lowest
6755: common ancestor is the ordinary tree property.
6756: Lemma A.12 (Top marked ancestor and complete-packet shell). In a packet tree from Lemma A.11,
6757: contract every maximal descendant subtree carrying no distinguished label into its exact parent-normalized
6758: decoration, and suppress every maximal unary chain into its exact ordered transfer product. The distin-
6759: guished labels are the two curvature roots and the zero, one or two active g marks. They have a unique
6760: lowest common ancestor v⊤ ; let j be its conditional scale. Call v⊤ the top marked ancestor.
6761:     All vertices strictly above v⊤ are unmarked. The complete coefficient created at v⊤ includes every
6762: contracted side decoration with its canonical reported support. Decompose these whole coefficients by their
6763: full reported-support shell k using a scalar translation- and reflection-invariant selector. The selected
6764: class is therefore a complete BRS/reflection packet with every denominator and normalization contact
6765: still inside it.

6773: Proof. Contraction of an unlabelled descendant is its exact conditional value, so it retains its canonical
6774: reported support. Suppression of a unary chain is the exact ordered transfer product. After these
6775: operations, a tree with at most four distinguished leaves has only a fixed number of essential branching
6776: edges, while all arbitrarily long unary portions are already contained in the parity transfer product. The
6777: unique lowest common ancestor of all labels is an ordinary tree invariant. If a g derivative lands on a
6778: coarser conditional map, its mark is attached at that map and therefore moves v⊤ to that scale. Hence
6779: no marked vertex can occur above v⊤ .

7380: The cellwise shell series and its first two g derivatives converge with one uniform majorant in cutoff,
7381: volume, preceding weak depth and the chosen weak scale r. Consequently the derivative bounds for the
7382: total separated functional extend to crossing-cell boundaries by finite-volume C 2 continuity.

7384: Proof. Work first at finite directed depth M and inside one crossing cell of Definition A.6. Apply the
7385: exact marked conditional forest. If the top marked ancestor is a weak conditional vertex of scale j,
7386: Lemma A.14 gives the complete row at that scale. Every later weak transfer before the selected terminal
7387: factor is unmarked, and Theorem A.13 contracts the entire complete rooted packet in one norm. A
7388: coupling mark after the two source roots have joined is not omitted: it becomes another distinguished
7389: leaf and moves the top marked ancestor to its own scale.
7390:    If the top marked ancestor is the selected terminal factor, apply Theorem A.22. On a crossing cell
7391: this is the complete compact handoff packet, with the two differences created at the first-crossing scale
7392: and the remaining separation paid by the joint Haar–optical connector. On the no-crossing cell it is
7393: the finite-depth weak terminal packet on the fixed coarse torus. The latter tends to zero through two

7476:    The higher Sobolev order concerns internal compact-pivot derivatives. It does not increase the physical
7477: source-mark budget; those marks remain the fixed orders consumed by the boundary-to-center and Ward
7478: arguments. In the ordered-multijet realization of Lemma A.17, one Sobolev monomial uses at most

7480: directions retain distinct variables. Thus, for example, D12 D22 is represented by the ordered word (1, 1, 2, 2)
7481: rather than by a fourth derivative along a single flow.

7483: D      Ordinary periodic torons, seams, and wrapping templates
7484: The finite-volume model used throughout is the ordinary periodic product space GE(Λ) with periodic
7485: gauge transformations. It is a single, explicit lattice model; no twisted boundary condition and no sum
7486: over nontrivial principal-bundle sectors is part of the construction. Since the target spacetime R4 is
7487: contractible, this restriction is compatible with the theorem’s local continuum scope.
7488:   Define the compact set of ordinary periodic flat holonomies
7489: 
7490:                           TG := {(h1 , h2 , h3 , h4 ) ∈ G4 : [hµ , hν ] = e for all µ, ν}.
7491: 
7492: Lemma D.1 (Tree-gauge reduction of ordinary periodic flat fields). Every flat configuration in the
7493: ordinary periodic link model is gauge equivalent to a seam representative whose interior links are the
7494: identity and whose four fundamental seam holonomies form an element of TG . Conversely every element
7495: of TG defines such a flat periodic configuration. The remaining gauge action is simultaneous conjugation
7496: of the four holonomies.

7492: Lemma D.1 (Tree-gauge reduction of ordinary periodic flat fields). Every flat configuration in the
7493: ordinary periodic link model is gauge equivalent to a seam representative whose interior links are the
7494: identity and whose four fundamental seam holonomies form an element of TG . Conversely every element
7495: of TG defines such a flat periodic configuration. The remaining gauge action is simultaneous conjugation

7505: a flat seam representative from every commuting four-tuple. A gauge transformation at the tree root
7506: conjugates all four holonomies simultaneously, and all other gauge freedom has already been fixed.

7518: order. Compactness gives a finite subcover. There are four seam directions and a finite aligned-reflection
7519: family, so taking a finite maximum gives the common constants.

7483: D      Ordinary periodic torons, seams, and wrapping templates
7484: The finite-volume model used throughout is the ordinary periodic product space GE(Λ) with periodic
7485: gauge transformations. It is a single, explicit lattice model; no twisted boundary condition and no sum
7486: over nontrivial principal-bundle sectors is part of the construction. Since the target spacetime R4 is
7487: contractible, this restriction is compatible with the theorem’s local continuum scope.
7488:   Define the compact set of ordinary periodic flat holonomies
7489: 
7490:                           TG := {(h1 , h2 , h3 , h4 ) ∈ G4 : [hµ , hν ] = e for all µ, ν}.
7491: 
7492: Lemma D.1 (Tree-gauge reduction of ordinary periodic flat fields). Every flat configuration in the
7493: ordinary periodic link model is gauge equivalent to a seam representative whose interior links are the
7494: identity and whose four fundamental seam holonomies form an element of TG . Conversely every element
7495: of TG defines such a flat periodic configuration. The remaining gauge action is simultaneous conjugation
7496: of the four holonomies.
7497: 
7498: 
7499:                                                           110

7501: Proof. Choose a maximal lattice tree in a fundamental periodic cell and gauge its links to the identity.
7502: Flatness makes the holonomy of a path depend only on its homotopy class. The four links closing the
7503: fundamental cycles therefore supply holonomies hµ . The commutator loop of two fundamental cycles
7504: is a product of plaquette boundaries, so flatness gives [hµ , hν ] = e. Reversing this construction gives
7505: a flat seam representative from every commuting four-tuple. A gauge transformation at the tree root
7506: conjugates all four holonomies simultaneously, and all other gauge freedom has already been fixed.
7507: Lemma D.2 (Finite toron and seam atlas). For every fixed compact G, the set TG has a finite cover by
7508: ambient product-coordinate neighborhoods on which every block word, Karcher word, coarea factor, Haar
7509: factor, reflected word, and source word used in the proof is analytic in the local variables and in the toron
7510: parameters. All derivatives through order 2sG + 3 have a finite common bound. The aligned coordinate
7511: reflections and seam placements add only a finite template family.
7512: Proof. The commutator equations define a closed subset of compact G4 , hence TG is compact. Around
7513: each of its points choose ordinary product charts in the ambient manifold G4 ; no quotient chart or
7514: classification of singular conjugacy classes is required. In tree gauge, every local factor is a fixed finite
7515: composition of multiplication, inversion, the local exponential/logarithm, and the Karcher implicit
7516: function, with some arguments left- or right-multiplied by the fixed seam holonomies. These maps are
7517: analytic on a sufficiently small product neighborhood and have finite Cauchy bounds through the declared
7518: order. Compactness gives a finite subcover. There are four seam directions and a finite aligned-reflection
7519: family, so taking a finite maximum gives the common constants.
7520: Theorem D.3 (Ordinary-periodic toron and optical admission). The complete full-alias right-inverse,
7521: normal-section, optical-coercivity, Haar-minorization, owner, and boundary-to-center estimates hold
7522: uniformly over every flat toron background in TG , every admitted seam and aligned-reflection template,
7523: and every periodic wrapping certificate. The acoustic toron/background variables remain explicit and are
7524: never inverted. No constant uniform over different groups is asserted.
7525: Proof. Lift a seam representative to the universal covering lattice. A flat background is gauged to the
7526: identity there; deck translation in direction µ acts on Lie-algebra fields by the unitary operator Ad(hµ ).
7527: Since the hµ commute, these unitary operators are simultaneously diagonalizable over the complexified
7528: color space. On each joint color eigenspace the folded block and optical symbols are therefore the
7529: identity-background, color-free symbols evaluated at a real quasi-momentum shifted by the corresponding

7537: complement is inverted.
7538:    Lemma D.2 supplies the nonlinear, coarea, Haar, and marked derivative bounds uniformly over a finite

6045: Let Yr (f ) be the tangent-normalized, Wick-centered scalar curvature source at physical scale r, with the

6047: unit scale and dilated with the source so that its dimensionless support remains fixed and lies strictly in
6048: positive Euclidean time.

6055: The value is independent of r in the normalized frame.
6056: Proof. At the Gaussian fixed point the centered scalar curvature composite creates a two-curvature Fock
6057: vector. In Fourier variables its squared norm is an integral of the squared symmetrized two-particle
6058: amplitude against the positive transverse curvature spectral density. That density is nonzero on an open
6059: set, so choosing a positive-time profile whose Fourier transform is nonzero there gives a strictly positive
6060: integral. Reflection positivity identifies the integral with the displayed OS form. Scale covariance of the
6061: tangent-normalized source makes the value independent of r.

1:  Yang–Mills Existence, Mass Gap, and Nontriviality
2: for Fixed Compact Simple Gauge Groups via Karcher
3:       Blocking and Exact Haar–Pivot Matching

13:                                                      Abstract
14:         Let G be a fixed compact connected simple Lie group and let ρ be a fixed faithful finite-dimensional
15:     unitary representation. We construct four-dimensional pure G Yang–Mills theory as a zero-spacing,
16:     infinite-volume limit of reflection-positive Wilson lattice measures. The Wilson plaquette function
17:     is normalized at the identity to the background quadratic form used to define the coupling u = g 2 .
18:     A weighted forty-nine-path Karcher block, complete sixteen-alias normal section, charged chart-
19:     complement partition, and permanent rooted domain produce a uniform scale-two map on a centered
20:     invariant graph,
21:                                                  11 CA (G) log 2
22:                                u(2L) = u(L) +                    u(L)2 + OG (u(L)3 ).
23:                                                      24π 2
24: 
25:     The complete linear block certificate is independent of color and tensors with Ig ; all nonlinear
26:     constants are finite and may depend on G. At the physical handoff, a group-dependent product
27:     Sobolev order 2sG > dG /2 gives a Peter–Weyl Fourier–Wiener landing for the exact one-pivot and
28:     multipivot densities. Separated Haar pivots contract nonvacuum compact flux, optical covariance
29:     contracts normal-field stretches, and all remaining vertices retain their support charge. A single
30:     marked replica–BKAR forest then gives a boundary-to-center two-root connector and one physical
31:     clustering rate. General invariant-jet transport, displacement and stress Ward identities, finite
32:     dimension-four mixing, and a dimension-six rotation regulator yield a tempered, reflection-positive,
33:     translation-continuous, O(4)-covariant Schwinger family. Osterwalder–Schrader reconstruction gives a
34:     positive-energy Poincaré-covariant local theory with unique vacuum and a strictly positive Hamiltonian
35:     gap. Endpoint-safe curvature-source transport produces a nonzero local vector with positive spectral
36:     mass in a finite interval above the gap. The theorem concerns the local gauge-invariant curvature-jet
37:     observable theory. No estimate is asserted uniformly over the class of compact simple groups.

91: 1     The compact-simple-group theorem and proof boundary
92: 1.1    Problem and result
93: The constructive Yang–Mills problem asks for a nontrivial quantum gauge field theory on R4 satisfying
94: the Osterwalder–Schrader and Wightman requirements and possessing a strictly positive mass gap [1].
95: The physical scale, thermodynamic limit, zero lattice spacing, Euclidean covariance, local spectral content,
96: and non-abelian field geometry must be controlled in one compatible construction.
97:    Fix throughout a compact connected simple Lie group G, a faithful unitary representation ρ : G → U (Vρ )
98: of dimension dρ , and a positive normalization of the invariant quadratic form for which the adjoint color
99: factor is denoted CA (G). The Wilson plaquette function is
100:                                                         1
101:                                                                     
102:                                    wG,ρ (U ) = cρ 1 −     Re Tr ρ(U ) ,
103:                                                        dρ
104: where cρ > 0 is chosen once so that the Hessian at the identity is the quadratic form used by the
105: background-field coupling coordinate u = g 2 . This choice is a finite linear normalization of the bare
106: Wilson coupling and is part of the fixed-group input register.
107: Theorem 1.1 (Four-dimensional Yang–Mills construction for fixed compact simple G). For every fixed
108: pair (G, ρ) as above, there is a directed sequence of reflection-positive Wilson lattice measures, with
109: microscopic spacing tending to zero and periodic volume tending to infinity, whose local gauge-invariant
110: curvature-jet Schwinger functions converge to a nontrivial Euclidean field theory on R4 .
111:    The limiting Schwinger family is tempered, permutation symmetric, reflection positive, translation
112: continuous, exponentially clustering, and O(4) covariant. Osterwalder–Schrader reconstruction yields a
113: positive-energy Poincaré-covariant local theory with a unique vacuum and a number m⋆ (G) > 0 such that
114:                                               H Ω⊥ ≥ m⋆ (G).
115: On the centered invariant weak trajectory, the exact scale-two coupling map is
116:                                             11 CA (G) log 2
117:                            u(2L) = u(L) +                   u(L)2 + OG (u(L)3 ),
118:                                                  24π 2
119: with uniform volume and cutoff control in the permanent weak regime. The reconstructed theory contains
120: a nonzero reflected local curvature vector and positive spectral mass in a finite interval [m⋆ (G), M⋆ (G)].
121: 1.2    Fixed-group conventions and finite register
122: Write g = Lie(G) and dG = dim G. All Haar measures are normalized. The finite-volume approximants
123: use the ordinary periodic link space GE(Λ) with periodic gauge transformations; no twisted boundary
124: condition or nontrivial finite-volume bundle sector is summed. This is sufficient for the claimed local
125: theory on the contractible space R4 . The bi-invariant metric, exponential coordinates, Laplacian ∆G ,
126: Casimir eigenvalues, and operator norms are those determined by the declared quadratic form. In
127: curvature formulas, Tr(XY ) denotes this normalized invariant bilinear pairing; raw representation traces
128: are written Tr ρ(·). Let                     

132: The fixed-group constant register CG consists of the finite quantities used below: the injectivity and
133: convexity radii, a positive local Karcher radius, finite holomorphic BCH and logarithm bounds through
134: order 2sG + 3, the faithful-action Hessian and complement charge, the Peter–Weyl constant, the finite
135: ordinary-periodic toron/seam template register, the adjoint color factor, and the finite invariant-jet
136: dimensions through the retained degree. Every statement called uniform is uniform in cutoff, volume,
137: directed depth, periodic wrapping, admitted exterior histories, and the finite reflected template family,
138: but its constants may depend on CG .

163: 1.3     Proof architecture
164: The proof has four stages. First, the exact Karcher block is placed in a complete full-alias normal chart
165: and combined with a charged chart-complement partition, permanent analytic domain, marked connected
166: logarithm, Wick-stable jet block, and centered invariant graph. The folded spacetime symbol tensors
167: with Ig , while all nonlinear estimates are admitted by the finite register CG .
168:    Second, exact background-scheme conjugacy identifies the universal marginal coefficient      and a directed
169: schedule reaches the compact matching scale. The higher product Sobolev operator p (1 − ∆p )sG replaces
170:                                                                                        Q
171: the rank-one order and supplies the fixed-G Fourier–Wiener rows.
172:    Third, complete Haar and optical variables are kept in one partition function. A mixed replica–
173: BKAR forest makes every root-to-root path pay separated Haar contraction, optical covariance decay,
174: or pre-existing support charge. Ordinary-periodic toron backgrounds, seams, reflections, and wrapping
175: certificates enter only through the finite template theorem of Appendix D.
176:    Finally, a least-label source forest transports the complete invariant-jet system. The degree-four block
177: is group-independent because a compact simple Lie algebra has a unique invariant symmetric bilinear
178: form; possible cubic color tensors first enter the finite dimension-six block. Ward identities, rotation-defect
179: estimates, reflection-positive recombination, and Osterwalder–Schrader reconstruction give covariance,
180: the unique vacuum, the gap, and noncollapse.

225: 1.5     Self-contained relation to the rank-one construction
226: The companion SU(2) manuscript [14] is a completely expanded rank-one specialization and a useful
227: independent check. It is not a logical input to Theorem 1.1: every common analytic theorem used here is
228: stated and proved in this paper, and every new fixed-group admission is printed below. Appendix F gives
229: a theorem-level specialization map so that a referee can identify which arguments are color-free, which
230: tensor with Ig , and which require new group data.
231: 1.6     Scope
232: The theorem concerns the local gauge-invariant curvature-jet observable sector for each fixed compact
233: connected simple G. It does not assert a bound uniform over groups, a simultaneous large-rank limit,
234: control of every extended Wilson-line or generalized-symmetry sector, or uniqueness of the continuum
235: limit outside the directed construction. Quotient groups are included through their actual compact link
236: group. The construction uses only the ordinary periodic finite-volume sector and retains all flat toron
237: backgrounds occurring in that sector; no claim about a sum over twisted boundary conditions is made or

===== finite spectral window HIT 2 source line 6043 =====
5973: then produces the positive- energy Poincaré representation. No unitary action of a Euclidean rotation
5974: that changes the reflection hyperplane is assumed on the intermediate OS quotient.

5976: 9     Continuum reconstruction and spectral gap
5977: 9.1    Osterwalder–Schrader reconstruction and clustering gap
5978: The common Schwinger functional Let D be the countable algebra generated by positive-time
5979: translates of the bounded positive-scale cylinders and the normalized finite local source fields constructed
5980: by the marked hierarchy. The multiscale source-tree theorem supplies a projective Schwinger functional
5981: S on D with the following properties:
5982: (S1) temperedness and continuity in every Schwartz test tensor;

5992: Theorem 9.1 (Continuous OS reconstruction). Let Θ be time reflection and D+ the positive-time
5993: subalgebra. The form

5996: and a strongly continuous self-adjoint contraction semigroup
5997:

6000: Proof. Reflection positivity gives the positive form and the null ideal N = {F : ∥F ∥OS = 0}. Positive-time
6001: translations preserve D+ for sufficiently small translations on each compactly supported word and define
6002: contractions on the quotient; the usual time-zero limiting argument extends them to all t ≥ 0. For a core
6003: vector [F ],
6004:                                         2
6005:                       ∥T (t)[F ] − [F ]∥ = S(ΘF τ2t F ) − 2ℜS(ΘF τt F ) + S(ΘF F ),
6006: which tends to zero by translation continuity. Contractivity and density give strong continuity on H.
6007: Reflection symmetry makes the semigroup self-adjoint, and the spectral theorem gives T (t) = e−tH with
6008: H ≥ 0.

6032: Theorem 9.3 (Unique vacuum and complete local mass gap). The OS Hamiltonian satisfies
6033:

6036: The vacuum is unique.
6037: Proof. The centered classes [F ] − S(F )Ω, F ∈ D+ , are dense in Ω⊥ . Lemma 9.2 says that the spectral
6038: projection EH ([0, m − ε]) annihilates this dense set for every ε > 0; hence it vanishes on Ω⊥ . This proves
6039: the absence of spectrum in (0, m).

6041: then shows that EH ({0})H = CΩ. Thus the vacuum is unique and (169) holds.

6044: 10.1     Endpoint interpolation and reflected noncollapse
6045: Let Yr (f ) be the tangent-normalized, Wick-centered scalar curvature source at physical scale r, with the
6046: source root parallel transported in the normalized finite-jet frame. The test profile f is chosen once at
6047: unit scale and dilated with the source so that its dimensionless support remains fixed and lies strictly in
6048: positive Euclidean time.
6049: Lemma 10.1 (Positive Gaussian OS norm). There is such a profile f for which
6050: 
6051:                                     V0 (f ) := S0 ΘYr (f ) Yr (f ) > 0.
6052:                                                                   
6053: 
6054: 
6055: The value is independent of r in the normalized frame.
6056: Proof. At the Gaussian fixed point the centered scalar curvature composite creates a two-curvature Fock
6057: vector. In Fourier variables its squared norm is an integral of the squared symmetrized two-particle
6058: amplitude against the positive transverse curvature spectral density. That density is nonzero on an open
6059: set, so choosing a positive-time profile whose Fourier transform is nonzero there gives a strictly positive
6060: integral. Reflection positivity identifies the integral with the displayed OS form. Scale covariance of the
6061: tangent-normalized source makes the value independent of r.

6348: 11     Completion for arbitrary fixed compact simple gauge groups
6349: Theorem 11.1 (Completion of the fixed-group construction). For every fixed compact connected simple
6350: G and faithful unitary representation ρ, the group register CG is finite and all hypotheses consumed by
6351: Sections 2–10 are satisfied. Consequently Theorem 1.1 holds.
6352: Proof. Compactness and the analytic bi-invariant geometry give the Karcher, logarithm, coarea, and
6353: faithful-action constants of Section 2. The complete folded block symbol is spacetime-only and tensors
6354: with Ig , so the strict right-inverse and optical coercivity constants are unchanged. The chart complement
6355: is charged by the faithful Wilson action and the irregular activity is exponentially small. The permanent
6356: weak map, centered graph, and BPHZL packet use the same finite-dimensional Lie-algebra tensor calculus,
6357: with the universal color factor CA (G).
6358:    The Peter–Weyl spectral sum converges at the fixed order sG , and the higher hybrid-polydisc argument
6359: supplies the one-pivot nuclear and multipivot Fourier–Wiener rows. The ordinary-periodic toron, seam,
6360: reflection, and wrapping templates are admitted by Theorem D.3; no twisted sector or finite-volume bundle
6361: sum is used. The invariant-jet theorem supplies the complete low-dimensional mixing and dimension-six
6362: regulator spaces. The joint Haar–optical forest, source transport, Ward hierarchy, OS reconstruction,
6363: and noncollapse theorem then apply with constants depending on G but uniform in cutoff, volume, and
6364: directed depth. Appendix F, specifically Table 3, records every independent entry of CG , the first proof
6365: call at which its G-dependence enters, and the subsequent consumers. Thus no additional estimate
6366: uniform in G is hidden in this completion step: after those finite calls, each remaining mechanism is
6367: color-free, tensors with Ig , or takes a finite maximum in a registered finite-dimensional space.
6368: Corollary 11.2 (The SU(N ) family). For every fixed integer N ≥ 2, the theorem holds for G = SU(N )
6369: in the defining representation, and the scale-two map is
6370:                                                11N log 2

6372:                                                  24π 2
6373: No estimate uniform as N → ∞ is asserted.
6374: Remark 11.3 (Logical scope). The construction is a fixed-group theorem. The directed subsequence,
6375: curvature-jet observable scope, and absence of a large-rank uniformity claim are part of the statement,
6376: not omitted conclusions.

6419: Lemma A.3 (Exact even-contact division of complete packets). For every complete packet class λ,
6420: the one-open-leg coefficient vanishes by global color invariance. The renormalized two-open-leg packet is
6421: reflection even, vanishes at p = 0, and admits the exact second-order division
6422:                                                       X

6424:                                                        α,β

6426: For every 0 < a′ < a0 , the rooted norm of Sλ is bounded by the second support moment of the same
6427: complete packet at exponent a0 .
6428:    If the complete packet is represented at weak scale i, the two displayed difference factors carry the
6429: canonical relative factor
6430:                                              (r/ℓi )2 = 2−2i                                       (189)
6431: with respect to the fixed physical source scale r. This factor belongs to the whole packet. It is not assigned
6432: to a chosen branch or attachment cut.
6433: Proof. The selector is inserted only after the exact normalized packet is formed and is scalar under color,
6434: translation and aligned reflection. Hence every selected sum is again a complete packet. Its one-open-leg
6435: coefficient is an invariant adjoint vector and therefore vanishes. Global contact subtraction is allocated
6436: after the disjoint packet decomposition, so each selected renormalized coefficient vanishes at p = 0.
6437: Reflection gives Tλ (−p) = Tλ (p) and therefore removes the linear Taylor term. The integral Taylor
6438: formula, or equivalently finite Laurent telescoping in the variables eipα , gives (188). Two momentum
6439: derivatives insert two support displacements, absorbed by the unused exponent a0 − a′ .
6440:    The divided packet has no reflection-even scalar or rank-two local component of engineering dimension
6441: four or five. Lemmas 5.22 and 5.23 therefore place its local projection in the stable degree-six block; this
6442: observation will be used only for stable membership, not to estimate the nonlocal packet.
6443:    The scale factor in (189) is instead purely geometric. In the tangent-normalized profile coordinates, one
6444: lattice difference at representation scale ℓi = 2i r is (r/ℓi ) times the corresponding unit-scale difference.
6445: The two explicit factors in (188) therefore contribute exactly (r/ℓi )2 = 2−2i . The two support moments
6446: of Sλ are absorbed by the unused rooted exponent. This argument applies to the whole packet, local and
6447: nonlocal together. Since the division identity holds for the entire C 2 family, zero, one or two g derivatives
6448: do not remove the two explicit differences.

6453: Lemma A.4 (g-marked one-scale admission). There is g0 > 0 such that, for real 0 ≤ g ≤ g0 , the complete
6454: normalized interaction and all declared source/defect marks are twice continuously differentiable in the
6455: permanent regulator space. An undistinguished non-Gaussian child has parent-normalized row
6456: 
6457:                                                  η0 (g) ≤ Cg,
6458: 
6459: while a child carrying one or two distinguished g derivatives has row
6460: 
6461:                                               η1 (g) + η2 (g) ≤ C.
6462: 
6463: The exactly recombined large-field/irregular row and its first two g derivatives extend continuously by zero
6464:                                              2
6465: at g = 0 and are bounded by Cm g −pm e−c/g for m ≤ 2. No claim that an attached odd child is O(g 2 ) is
6466: made.
6467: Proof. In the scale-normalized Gaussian coordinate, the cubic Yang–Mills species starts at order g, the
6468: quartic and other non-Gaussian even species start at order g 2 , and the common species tube controls
6469: all field and source marks. Thus an unmarked species row is Cg, while one or two explicit g derivatives
6470: remove at most the corresponding powers and give a finite row. The regulator allocations are additive
6471: under multiplication, and the Gram and Pruefer estimates in the summed replica–BKAR theorem are
6472: insensitive to the parity of an attached tensor. The ordinary parent-normalized child recursion therefore
6473: closes for Cg0 below its printed threshold, with two fixed derivative marks handled by Leibniz. The
6474: C 3 -flat irregular estimate in u = g 2 gives the displayed first and second g-derivative rows after the chain
6475: rule. In particular the model child gA3 differentiated once by a covariance edge is correctly admitted as
6476: an O(g) child, and one g derivative of that child is O(1).

91: 1     The compact-simple-group theorem and proof boundary
92: 1.1    Problem and result
93: The constructive Yang–Mills problem asks for a nontrivial quantum gauge field theory on R4 satisfying
94: the Osterwalder–Schrader and Wightman requirements and possessing a strictly positive mass gap [1].
95: The physical scale, thermodynamic limit, zero lattice spacing, Euclidean covariance, local spectral content,
96: and non-abelian field geometry must be controlled in one compatible construction.
97:    Fix throughout a compact connected simple Lie group G, a faithful unitary representation ρ : G → U (Vρ )
98: of dimension dρ , and a positive normalization of the invariant quadratic form for which the adjoint color
99: factor is denoted CA (G). The Wilson plaquette function is
100:                                                         1
101:                                                                     
102:                                    wG,ρ (U ) = cρ 1 −     Re Tr ρ(U ) ,
103:                                                        dρ
104: where cρ > 0 is chosen once so that the Hessian at the identity is the quadratic form used by the
105: background-field coupling coordinate u = g 2 . This choice is a finite linear normalization of the bare
106: Wilson coupling and is part of the fixed-group input register.
107: Theorem 1.1 (Four-dimensional Yang–Mills construction for fixed compact simple G). For every fixed
108: pair (G, ρ) as above, there is a directed sequence of reflection-positive Wilson lattice measures, with
109: microscopic spacing tending to zero and periodic volume tending to infinity, whose local gauge-invariant
110: curvature-jet Schwinger functions converge to a nontrivial Euclidean field theory on R4 .
111:    The limiting Schwinger family is tempered, permutation symmetric, reflection positive, translation
112: continuous, exponentially clustering, and O(4) covariant. Osterwalder–Schrader reconstruction yields a
113: positive-energy Poincaré-covariant local theory with a unique vacuum and a number m⋆ (G) > 0 such that
114:                                               H Ω⊥ ≥ m⋆ (G).
115: On the centered invariant weak trajectory, the exact scale-two coupling map is
116:                                             11 CA (G) log 2
117:                            u(2L) = u(L) +                   u(L)2 + OG (u(L)3 ),
118:                                                  24π 2
119: with uniform volume and cutoff control in the permanent weak regime. The reconstructed theory contains
120: a nonzero reflected local curvature vector and positive spectral mass in a finite interval [m⋆ (G), M⋆ (G)].
121: 1.2    Fixed-group conventions and finite register
122: Write g = Lie(G) and dG = dim G. All Haar measures are normalized. The finite-volume approximants
123: use the ordinary periodic link space GE(Λ) with periodic gauge transformations; no twisted boundary
124: condition or nontrivial finite-volume bundle sector is summed. This is sufficient for the claimed local
125: theory on the contractible space R4 . The bi-invariant metric, exponential coordinates, Laplacian ∆G ,
126: Casimir eigenvalues, and operator norms are those determined by the declared quadratic form. In
127: curvature formulas, Tr(XY ) denotes this normalized invariant bilinear pairing; raw representation traces
128: are written Tr ρ(·). Let                     
129:                                               dG                    dG
130:                                       sG :=       + 1,      2sG >       .
131:                                                4                      2
132: The fixed-group constant register CG consists of the finite quantities used below: the injectivity and
133: convexity radii, a positive local Karcher radius, finite holomorphic BCH and logarithm bounds through
134: order 2sG + 3, the faithful-action Hessian and complement charge, the Peter–Weyl constant, the finite
135: ordinary-periodic toron/seam template register, the adjoint color factor, and the finite invariant-jet
136: dimensions through the retained degree. Every statement called uniform is uniform in cutoff, volume,
137: directed depth, periodic wrapping, admitted exterior histories, and the finite reflected template family,
138: but its constants may depend on CG .
139: Proposition 1.2 (Reflection-positive Wilson input). For every finite periodic lattice Λ and u > 0, in the
140: ordinary periodic link sector,
141:                                                 "                    #

149: is gauge invariant and reflection positive for coordinate reflections. Moreover wG,ρ ≥ 0, its zero set is the
150: identity, and on one fixed identity neighborhood
151:                                      cG ∥X∥2 ≤ wG,ρ (eX ) ≤ CG ∥X∥2 .

156: Proof. The normalized character satisfies Re Tr ρ(U ) ≤ dρ , with equality only when ρ(U ) = I; faithfulness
157: gives U = e. The Hessian statement follows from the chosen normalization and compactness. Up to a field-
158: independent constant, each plaquette factor is exp{t(χρ +χρ )} with t > 0. Its power series is a nonnegative
159: linear combination of characters of tensor products of ρ and ρ̄, and their irreducible decompositions
160: have nonnegative integer multiplicities. The standard character-expansion reflection-positivity argument
161: therefore applies.

163: 1.3     Proof architecture
164: The proof has four stages. First, the exact Karcher block is placed in a complete full-alias normal chart
165: and combined with a charged chart-complement partition, permanent analytic domain, marked connected
166: logarithm, Wick-stable jet block, and centered invariant graph. The folded spacetime symbol tensors
167: with Ig , while all nonlinear estimates are admitted by the finite register CG .
168:    Second, exact background-scheme conjugacy identifies the universal marginal coefficient      and a directed
169: schedule reaches the compact matching scale. The higher product Sobolev operator p (1 − ∆p )sG replaces
170:                                                                                        Q
171: the rank-one order and supplies the fixed-G Fourier–Wiener rows.
172:    Third, complete Haar and optical variables are kept in one partition function. A mixed replica–
173: BKAR forest makes every root-to-root path pay separated Haar contraction, optical covariance decay,
174: or pre-existing support charge. Ordinary-periodic toron backgrounds, seams, reflections, and wrapping
175: certificates enter only through the finite template theorem of Appendix D.
176:    Finally, a least-label source forest transports the complete invariant-jet system. The degree-four block
177: is group-independent because a compact simple Lie algebra has a unique invariant symmetric bilinear
178: form; possible cubic color tensors first enter the finite dimension-six block. Ward identities, rotation-defect
179: estimates, reflection-positive recombination, and Osterwalder–Schrader reconstruction give covariance,
180: the unique vacuum, the gap, and noncollapse.

183: Sobolev landing, periodic-toron template theorem, invariant-jet admission, matching constants, and
184: noncollapse source are internal.

225: 1.5     Self-contained relation to the rank-one construction
226: The companion SU(2) manuscript [14] is a completely expanded rank-one specialization and a useful
227: independent check. It is not a logical input to Theorem 1.1: every common analytic theorem used here is
228: stated and proved in this paper, and every new fixed-group admission is printed below. Appendix F gives
229: a theorem-level specialization map so that a referee can identify which arguments are color-free, which
230: tensor with Ig , and which require new group data.
231: 1.6     Scope
232: The theorem concerns the local gauge-invariant curvature-jet observable sector for each fixed compact
233: connected simple G. It does not assert a bound uniform over groups, a simultaneous large-rank limit,
234: control of every extended Wilson-line or generalized-symmetry sector, or uniqueness of the continuum
235: limit outside the directed construction. Quotient groups are included through their actual compact link
236: group. The construction uses only the ordinary periodic finite-volume sector and retains all flat toron
237: backgrounds occurring in that sector; no claim about a sum over twisted boundary conditions is made or
238: needed.
239: 
240: 2     Fixed-group Wilson geometry and Karcher blocking
241: Let Λ be a finite periodic four-dimensional lattice and use the reflection-positive Wilson measure of
242: Proposition 1.2. For each oriented retained link, use the straight two-link path with weight 7/8 and the
243: forty-eight signed-corner staples with weight 1/384 each. On a fixed strongly convex identity tube, define
244: the retained link as their weighted Karcher mean in the bi-invariant metric.
245: Lemma 2.1 (Fixed-group convex Karcher and holomorphic atlas). There are radii 0 < r0,G < r1,G
246: such that the squared-distance functions, product, inverse, exponential, logarithm, and weighted Karcher
247: critical point extend holomorphically to the corresponding local complexification tube. The Karcher Hessian
248: is bounded below there by a positive constant depending only on G. All derivatives through arbitrary
249: prescribed finite order are bounded on the smaller tube.
250: Proof. A compact Lie group with a bi-invariant analytic metric has positive injectivity and strong-convexity
251: radii. The sectional curvature is bounded because G is compact, and the Hessian comparison theorem
252: gives a positive lower Hessian bound on a sufficiently small ball. In a faithful matrix representation
253: the local complexification is an open neighborhood in the complexified group; multiplication, inversion,
254: exponential, and the principal logarithm are holomorphic there. The analytic implicit-function theorem
255: applied to the Karcher critical equation gives the holomorphic mean. Cauchy estimates on nested compact
256: tubes give every fixed derivative order; standard compact Lie-group analytic facts are summarized in
257: [15].
258: Proposition 2.2 (Exact block covariance and local submersion). The retained map is gauge covariant,
259: orientation covariant, signed-hypercubic covariant, and aligned-reflection covariant. Its linearization at
260: the flat connection has full retained rank. On one fixed weak tube it admits a finite-volume analytic
261: normal section whose matrix kernel and first three marked derivatives are exponentially local, uniformly
262: in volume.
263: Proof. Every path has the same endpoints, and the weighted squared-distance functional is invariant
264: under simultaneous left and right multiplication. Uniqueness of the Karcher minimizer gives gauge and
265: orientation covariance. The signed-corner path family is invariant under the lattice symmetry group.
266: At the flat point, the exact Fourier symbol is the color-free spacetime symbol tensored with Ig and has
267: acoustic value 2I ⊗ Ig and a uniformly gapped normal frame. The full-alias section and normal-fiber
268: contraction are proved in the later normal-chart chapters.
269: Proposition 2.3 (Exact coarea–fiber identity). At finite volume, in the normal fiber chart,
270: 
271:                                     dU = J0 det(I + DN P )−1 dW dζ,
272: 
273: 
274:                                                      5
275: 
276: where the determinant is the ordinary finite-dimensional determinant. The fixed linear determinant is
277: owned once as a scalar normalization, and the field-dependent logarithm is one connected analytic activity.

14:         Let G be a fixed compact connected simple Lie group and let ρ be a fixed faithful finite-dimensional
15:     unitary representation. We construct four-dimensional pure G Yang–Mills theory as a zero-spacing,
16:     infinite-volume limit of reflection-positive Wilson lattice measures. The Wilson plaquette function
17:     is normalized at the identity to the background quadratic form used to define the coupling u = g 2 .
18:     A weighted forty-nine-path Karcher block, complete sixteen-alias normal section, charged chart-
19:     complement partition, and permanent rooted domain produce a uniform scale-two map on a centered

25:     The complete linear block certificate is independent of color and tensors with Ig ; all nonlinear
26:     constants are finite and may depend on G. At the physical handoff, a group-dependent product
27:     Sobolev order 2sG > dG /2 gives a Peter–Weyl Fourier–Wiener landing for the exact one-pivot and
28:     multipivot densities. Separated Haar pivots contract nonvacuum compact flux, optical covariance
29:     contracts normal-field stretches, and all remaining vertices retain their support charge. A single
30:     marked replica–BKAR forest then gives a boundary-to-center two-root connector and one physical
31:     clustering rate. General invariant-jet transport, displacement and stress Ward identities, finite

34:     positive-energy Poincaré-covariant local theory with unique vacuum and a strictly positive Hamiltonian
35:     gap. Endpoint-safe curvature-source transport produces a nonzero local vector with positive spectral
36:     mass in a finite interval above the gap. The theorem concerns the local gauge-invariant curvature-jet

91: 1     The compact-simple-group theorem and proof boundary
92: 1.1    Problem and result
93: The constructive Yang–Mills problem asks for a nontrivial quantum gauge field theory on R4 satisfying
94: the Osterwalder–Schrader and Wightman requirements and possessing a strictly positive mass gap [1].
95: The physical scale, thermodynamic limit, zero lattice spacing, Euclidean covariance, local spectral content,
96: and non-abelian field geometry must be controlled in one compatible construction.
97:    Fix throughout a compact connected simple Lie group G, a faithful unitary representation ρ : G → U (Vρ )
98: of dimension dρ , and a positive normalization of the invariant quadratic form for which the adjoint color
99: factor is denoted CA (G). The Wilson plaquette function is
100:                                                         1
101:                                                                     
102:                                    wG,ρ (U ) = cρ 1 −     Re Tr ρ(U ) ,
103:                                                        dρ
104: where cρ > 0 is chosen once so that the Hessian at the identity is the quadratic form used by the
105: background-field coupling coordinate u = g 2 . This choice is a finite linear normalization of the bare
106: Wilson coupling and is part of the fixed-group input register.
107: Theorem 1.1 (Four-dimensional Yang–Mills construction for fixed compact simple G). For every fixed
108: pair (G, ρ) as above, there is a directed sequence of reflection-positive Wilson lattice measures, with
109: microscopic spacing tending to zero and periodic volume tending to infinity, whose local gauge-invariant
110: curvature-jet Schwinger functions converge to a nontrivial Euclidean field theory on R4 .
111:    The limiting Schwinger family is tempered, permutation symmetric, reflection positive, translation
112: continuous, exponentially clustering, and O(4) covariant. Osterwalder–Schrader reconstruction yields a
113: positive-energy Poincaré-covariant local theory with a unique vacuum and a number m⋆ (G) > 0 such that
114:                                               H Ω⊥ ≥ m⋆ (G).
115: On the centered invariant weak trajectory, the exact scale-two coupling map is
116:                                             11 CA (G) log 2
117:                            u(2L) = u(L) +                   u(L)2 + OG (u(L)3 ),
118:                                                  24π 2
119: with uniform volume and cutoff control in the permanent weak regime. The reconstructed theory contains
120: a nonzero reflected local curvature vector and positive spectral mass in a finite interval [m⋆ (G), M⋆ (G)].
121: 1.2    Fixed-group conventions and finite register
122: Write g = Lie(G) and dG = dim G. All Haar measures are normalized. The finite-volume approximants
123: use the ordinary periodic link space GE(Λ) with periodic gauge transformations; no twisted boundary
124: condition or nontrivial finite-volume bundle sector is summed. This is sufficient for the claimed local
125: theory on the contractible space R4 . The bi-invariant metric, exponential coordinates, Laplacian ∆G ,
126: Casimir eigenvalues, and operator norms are those determined by the declared quadratic form. In
127: curvature formulas, Tr(XY ) denotes this normalized invariant bilinear pairing; raw representation traces
128: are written Tr ρ(·). Let                     
129:                                               dG                    dG
130:                                       sG :=       + 1,      2sG >       .
131:                                                4                      2
132: The fixed-group constant register CG consists of the finite quantities used below: the injectivity and
133: convexity radii, a positive local Karcher radius, finite holomorphic BCH and logarithm bounds through
134: order 2sG + 3, the faithful-action Hessian and complement charge, the Peter–Weyl constant, the finite
135: ordinary-periodic toron/seam template register, the adjoint color factor, and the finite invariant-jet
136: dimensions through the retained degree. Every statement called uniform is uniform in cutoff, volume,
137: directed depth, periodic wrapping, admitted exterior histories, and the finite reflected template family,
138: but its constants may depend on CG .
139: Proposition 1.2 (Reflection-positive Wilson input). For every finite periodic lattice Λ and u > 0, in the
140: ordinary periodic link sector,
141:                                                 "                    #

149: is gauge invariant and reflection positive for coordinate reflections. Moreover wG,ρ ≥ 0, its zero set is the
150: identity, and on one fixed identity neighborhood
151:                                      cG ∥X∥2 ≤ wG,ρ (eX ) ≤ CG ∥X∥2 .

156: Proof. The normalized character satisfies Re Tr ρ(U ) ≤ dρ , with equality only when ρ(U ) = I; faithfulness
157: gives U = e. The Hessian statement follows from the chosen normalization and compactness. Up to a field-
158: independent constant, each plaquette factor is exp{t(χρ +χρ )} with t > 0. Its power series is a nonnegative
159: linear combination of characters of tensor products of ρ and ρ̄, and their irreducible decompositions
160: have nonnegative integer multiplicities. The standard character-expansion reflection-positivity argument
161: therefore applies.
162: 
163: 1.3     Proof architecture
164: The proof has four stages. First, the exact Karcher block is placed in a complete full-alias normal chart
165: and combined with a charged chart-complement partition, permanent analytic domain, marked connected
166: logarithm, Wick-stable jet block, and centered invariant graph. The folded spacetime symbol tensors
167: with Ig , while all nonlinear estimates are admitted by the finite register CG .
168:    Second, exact background-scheme conjugacy identifies the universal marginal coefficient      and a directed
169: schedule reaches the compact matching scale. The higher product Sobolev operator p (1 − ∆p )sG replaces
170:                                                                                        Q
171: the rank-one order and supplies the fixed-G Fourier–Wiener rows.
172:    Third, complete Haar and optical variables are kept in one partition function. A mixed replica–
173: BKAR forest makes every root-to-root path pay separated Haar contraction, optical covariance decay,
174: or pre-existing support charge. Ordinary-periodic toron backgrounds, seams, reflections, and wrapping
175: certificates enter only through the finite template theorem of Appendix D.
176:    Finally, a least-label source forest transports the complete invariant-jet system. The degree-four block
177: is group-independent because a compact simple Lie algebra has a unique invariant symmetric bilinear
178: form; possible cubic color tensors first enter the finite dimension-six block. Ward identities, rotation-defect
179: estimates, reflection-positive recombination, and Osterwalder–Schrader reconstruction give covariance,
180: the unique vacuum, the gap, and noncollapse.
181: 1.4     External inputs and exact proof boundary
182: Only the theorem-level inputs in Table 1 are imported. The fixed-group geometry, action charge, higher
183: Sobolev landing, periodic-toron template theorem, invariant-jet admission, matching constants, and
184: noncollapse source are internal.

225: 1.5     Self-contained relation to the rank-one construction
226: The companion SU(2) manuscript [14] is a completely expanded rank-one specialization and a useful
227: independent check. It is not a logical input to Theorem 1.1: every common analytic theorem used here is
228: stated and proved in this paper, and every new fixed-group admission is printed below. Appendix F gives
229: a theorem-level specialization map so that a referee can identify which arguments are color-free, which
230: tensor with Ig , and which require new group data.
231: 1.6     Scope
232: The theorem concerns the local gauge-invariant curvature-jet observable sector for each fixed compact
233: connected simple G. It does not assert a bound uniform over groups, a simultaneous large-rank limit,
234: control of every extended Wilson-line or generalized-symmetry sector, or uniqueness of the continuum
235: limit outside the directed construction. Quotient groups are included through their actual compact link
236: group. The construction uses only the ordinary periodic finite-volume sector and retains all flat toron
237: backgrounds occurring in that sector; no claim about a sum over twisted boundary conditions is made or
